## Enhancement Priority Matrix

```
                    IMPACT
                 Low │ Medium │  High
            ─────────┼────────┼──────────
       Low  │   11   │   9    │   3, 4   │  EFFORT
            │        │        │          │
    Medium  │   10   │  6, 8  │  1, 5    │
            │        │        │          │
      High  │   12   │  7, 2  │    2     │
            ─────────┴────────┴──────────

  Recommended order:
  Phase 1 (quick wins):   9 → 10 → 4 → 11
  Phase 2 (high value):   1 → 2  → 5 → 8
  Phase 3 (enterprise):   7 → 3  → 6 → 12
```

---

## Minimal Change Principle

All enhancements above are designed to slot into the existing architecture with **no breaking changes** to the current pipeline:

- New agents are added as **additional LangGraph nodes** — existing nodes are not modified.
- New connectors implement the `BaseConnector` interface — `ExecutorAgent` dispatch logic changes minimally.
- New config keys are **additive** — existing `.env` and `.toml` files continue to work unchanged.
- New API endpoints are **additions** — existing `/chat` and `/orchestrate` endpoints are untouched.



####################################################################################################
FILE: docs\guardrails.md
####################################################################################################

# Guardrails

The pipeline has **four independent guard layers**.  None of them call the LLM — they are all rule-based and therefore fast, deterministic, and zero-cost.

```
User question
      │
      ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────┐
 │  Layer 1: IntentChecker     │  Blocks empty / harmful / vague input
 └──────────────┬──────────────┘
                │ passes
                ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────┐
 │  LLM generates SQL          │
 └──────────────┬──────────────┘
                │
                ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────┐
 │  Layer 2: SQLReviewer       │  Blocks DML, checks structure + tables
 └──────────────┬──────────────┘
                │ passes
                ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────┐
 │  Layer 3: SQLValidator      │  AST parse, SELECT-only gate
 └──────────────┬──────────────┘
                │ passes
                ▼
 ┌─────────────────────────────┐
 │  Layer 4: ExecutorAgent     │  BEGIN DEFERRED + unconditional ROLLBACK
 └──────────────┬──────────────┘
                │
                ▼
          Result rows
```

---

## Layer 1 — IntentChecker

**File:** `genai/nl2sql_agent/intent_checker.py`  
**When:** Before anything reaches the LLM.

### Blocked inputs

| Type | Examples | Response |
|------|----------|----------|
| Empty question | `""`, `"   "` | "No question was provided…" |
| Disallowed action word | `"delete accounts"`, `"drop table"` | "⚠ Sorry, the keyword DELETE is not permitted…" |
| Too short / meaningless | `"pending"`, `"what is"` | "Your question is too short or unclear…" |
| Vague / ambiguous | `"give me data"`, `"please verify this"` | "Your question is unclear. Please rephrase…" |

### Disallowed action words (complete list)

```
delete  drop  insert  update  truncate  alter  create  replace
pragma  attach  detach  vacuum  modify  remove  erase  wipe  purge  destroy
```

### Customising messages

All user-facing strings live in `genai/prompt/intent_checker.yaml`.  Edit that file to change wording — no Python change required.

```yaml
# genai/prompt/intent_checker.yaml
messages:
  empty_question:      "No question was provided…"
  disallowed_action:   "⚠ Sorry, the keyword {blocked_word} is not permitted…"
  too_short:           "Your question is too short or unclear to process."
  clarification:       "Your question is unclear. Please rephrase with more detail."
```

### Adjusting the metric-term bypass list

Edit `_METRIC_TERMS` in `intent_checker.py` to add domain-specific terms that should bypass the too-short check:

```python
_METRIC_TERMS: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset({
    "age", "kyc", "balance", "maturity", "product", "branch", "circle",
    # ← add your terms here, lowercase
})
```

---

## Layer 2 — SQLReviewer

**File:** `genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_reviewer.py`  
**When:** After the LLM generates SQL.

### Checks (in order)

| Check | Trigger | Action |
|-------|---------|--------|
| Retry ceiling | `retry_count >= max_retries` (default 3) | Sets `error` field → Executor surfaces the message to user |
| Empty SQL | `sql_query == ""` | `retry_count += 1` → back to SQLWriter |
| Disallowed keyword | Any of INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/DROP/TRUNCATE/ALTER/CREATE/REPLACE/PRAGMA/ATTACH/DETACH/VACUUM | `retry_count += 1` → back to SQLWriter |
| No SELECT prefix | SQL doesn't start with `SELECT` | `retry_count += 1` → back to SQLWriter |
| Missing FROM | No `FROM` keyword found | `retry_count += 1` → back to SQLWriter |
| Unknown table | Any table in `FROM`/`JOIN` clauses doesn't exist in the `.db` | `retry_count += 1` → back to SQLWriter |

### Changing max retries

In `genai/nl2sql_agent/graph.py`:

```python
sql_reviewer = SQLReviewer(max_retries=3)   # ← change here
```

Or in `build_nl2sql_graph()`:

```python
def build_nl2sql_graph(..., max_review_retries: int = 3, ...):
```

### Customising feedback messages

All reviewer feedback strings live in `genai/prompt/sql_review.yaml`:

```yaml
feedback:
  empty_sql:        "The SQL Writer returned an empty query."
  no_select:        "The query must begin with SELECT."
  missing_from:     "The query is missing a FROM clause."
  disallowed_kw:    "Keyword {kw} is not allowed in compliance queries."
  unknown_tables:   "The following tables do not exist: {tables}."
  max_retries:      "⚠ The request could not be completed after {max_retries} attempts."
```

---

## Layer 3 — SQLValidator (sqlglot AST)

**File:** `genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_validator.py`  
**When:** After SQLReviewer passes.

### Checks

| Check | What it catches | Example |
|-------|----------------|---------|
| **Parse validity** | Syntax errors the regex reviewer missed | `SELECT * FORM tbl` (typo) |
| **Statement type** | Non-SELECT statements that regex missed | `EXPLAIN SELECT …` (type = `Command`) |
| **Dialect normalisation** | Vendor-specific syntax not valid in SQLite | Rewrites to canonical SQLite |

### Changing the dialect

```python
# genai/nl2sql_agent/graph.py
sql_validator = SQLValidator(dialect="sqlite")   # ← change here
```

### Bypassing (not recommended)

If sqlglot is not installed the node automatically skips and sets `sqlglot_valid=True`.

---

## Layer 4 — ExecutorAgent (read-only SQLite)

**File:** `genai/nl2sql_agent/executor_agent.py`  
**When:** After all prior layers pass.

### Read-only mechanism

```python
conn.execute("BEGIN DEFERRED")   # No write locks acquired
cursor = conn.execute(sql)       # SELECT only reaches here
# ...
conn.rollback()                  # Always — even if execute() succeeded
conn.close()
```

`BEGIN DEFERRED` means SQLite acquires no lock at all until the first read.  Because we always rollback, **no write can ever be committed** regardless of what SQL text reaches the executor.

### Row-limit policy

Controlled entirely by `genai/config/query.toml`:

```toml
[query]
enforce_default_limit = false   # true = auto-append LIMIT if SQL has none
default_limit         = 10      # rows appended when enforce=true
max_display_rows      = 50      # hard ceiling via fetchmany()
```

`max_display_rows` applies regardless of `enforce_default_limit` — it is a hard memory/performance ceiling enforced by `cursor.fetchmany(max_display_rows)`.

---

## Summary — What Each Layer Catches

| Attack / mistake | Layer 1 | Layer 2 | Layer 3 | Layer 4 |
|-----------------|---------|---------|---------|---------|
| `DELETE FROM …` | ✓ (action word) | ✓ (keyword check) | ✓ (non-SELECT AST) | ✓ (rollback) |
| `SELECT 1; DROP TABLE …` | — | ✓ (DROP keyword) | ✓ (multiple stmts) | ✓ (rollback) |
| SQL with typo | — | — | ✓ (ParseError) | — |
| SQL with wrong table | — | ✓ (table check) | — | — |
| Vague non-query | ✓ (vague check) | — | — | — |
| 10 MB result set | — | — | — | ✓ (fetchmany cap) |

# Compliance Chatbot — Documentation Index

> **Version 2.0.0** · LangGraph-powered NL-to-SQL compliance query engine for banking term-deposit products.

---

## Quick-start (30 seconds)

```powershell
# 1 — install dependencies (from project root)
pip install -r requirements.txt

# 2 — seed the database
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 2000

# 3 — start the backend  (always from project root)
python application/main.py --port 8668

# 4 — in a second terminal, start the UI
python application/run_ui.py
```

Open **http://127.0.0.1:5001** in a browser.  
FastAPI Swagger docs are at **http://127.0.0.1:8668/docs**.

---

## Documents in this folder

| File | What it covers |
|---|---|
| **[architecture.md](architecture.md)** | Folder layout, component map, data-flow diagram |
| **[agents.md](agents.md)** | The 6 LangGraph nodes — what each one does and how to extend them |
| **[guardrails.md](guardrails.md)** | All 4 guard layers: intent, review, AST validation, executor safety |
| **[logging.md](logging.md)** | Log configuration, output location, how to read and filter logs |
| **[add-database.md](add-database.md)** | Step-by-step: add a new SQLite database with minimal code changes |
| **[configuration.md](configuration.md)** | Every config file and `.env` key explained |
| **[api.md](api.md)** | REST endpoint reference with request / response examples |
| **[running.md](running.md)** | All three launch modes: `main.py`, `run_ui.py`, `Launcher.py` |

---

## Repository layout at a glance

```
COMPLIANCE-CHATBOT-DLH-V2/
├── application/          ← FastAPI backend + UI server + Launcher
├── genai/
│   ├── config/           ← TOML configuration files
│   ├── core/             ← exceptions, schemas, model service, processor
│   ├── nl2sql_agent/     ← six LangGraph pipeline nodes
│   └── prompt/           ← YAML prompt files (one per agent)
├── utils/                ← shared helpers, config loader, registry, logger
├── data/
│   ├── comp_db/          ← SQLite database files
│   ├── logs/             ← rotating log files
│   └── seed_synthetic_data.py
├── tests/                ← pytest test suite
└── docs/                 ← ← you are here
```

---

## Key concepts

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **AgentState** | Single `TypedDict` that flows through every node; each node writes only its own keys |
| **LangGraph** | Directed state-graph framework; nodes are Python callables, edges are conditional routes |
| **NL2SQL** | Natural Language to SQL — translating a user question into a SQLite SELECT statement |
| **databases.toml** | The single file where you register new databases and tables |
| **YAML prompts** | All LLM instructions live in `genai/prompt/*.yaml`; no strings are hardcoded in Python |
| **DLH** | On-prem LLM inference server (OpenAI-compatible API); configured in `.env` |

---

## Minimum required environment variables (`.env`)

```dotenv
MODEL__PROVIDER=dlh
MODEL__MODEL_NAME=google/gemma-4-31b-it
MODEL__DLH_BASE_URL=http://<your-dlh-host>:9000/api/v1
MODEL__DLH_API_KEY=EMPTY
DATABASE__DB_FOLDER=data/comp_db
DATABASE__DB_NAME=COMP_REPORTS.db
```

See **[configuration.md](configuration.md)** for the full reference.



####################################################################################################
FILE: docs\logging.md
####################################################################################################

# Logging

## Overview

All logging is bootstrapped through a single factory function:

```python
from utils.logger import get_logger
LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.my_module")
```

**First call** configures the entire Python logging system from `genai/config/logging.toml`.  
Subsequent calls just return the named logger from the standard library cache.

---

## Log Destinations

| Handler | Level | Where | Format |
|---------|-------|-------|--------|
| `console` | INFO | stdout | `asctime | LEVEL | name | module:lineno | message` |
| `rotating_file` | DEBUG | `data/logs/compliance_bot.log` | same |

The file handler uses `RotatingFileHandler` — it rolls over at **10 MB** and keeps **10 backups**.

---

## Log File Location

The log directory is always `<project_root>/data/logs/` regardless of which directory you run the server from.

This is enforced in `utils/logger.py`:

```python
_PROJECT_ROOT: Path = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
# ...
resolved_log_dir = raw_dir if raw_dir.is_absolute() else _PROJECT_ROOT / raw_dir
```

So even if you `cd application && python main.py`, the log file ends up at:

```
COMPLIANCE-CHATBOT-DLH-V2/
└── data/
    └── logs/
        └── compliance_bot.log    ← always here
```

---

## Configuration File

`genai/config/logging.toml` — edit this to change log behaviour.

```toml
[logging]
version                  = 1
disable_existing_loggers = false

[logging.formatters.standard]
format  = "%(asctime)s | %(levelname)-8s | %(name)s | %(module)s:%(lineno)d | %(message)s"
datefmt = "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S"

[logging.handlers.console]
class     = "logging.StreamHandler"
level     = "INFO"       # ← change to DEBUG to see all messages in terminal
formatter = "standard"
stream    = "ext://sys.stdout"

[logging.handlers.rotating_file]
class       = "logging.handlers.RotatingFileHandler"
level       = "DEBUG"    # ← file always gets DEBUG and above
formatter   = "standard"
filename    = "data/logs/compliance_bot.log"  # overwritten at runtime with absolute path
maxBytes    = 10485760   # 10 MB
backupCount = 10
encoding    = "utf-8"
delay       = true       # don't create the file until first write

[logging.loggers.compliance_bot]
level     = "DEBUG"
handlers  = ["console", "rotating_file"]
propagate = false        # ← don't bubble to the root logger

[logging.loggers.uvicorn]
level     = "INFO"
handlers  = ["console"]
propagate = false

[logging.root]
level    = "WARNING"
handlers = ["console"]
```

---

## Changing Log Level

### Option A — `.env` (recommended)

```ini
LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG
```

This sets the uvicorn log level via `AppSettings.log_level`.

### Option B — `logging.toml` console handler

```toml
[logging.handlers.console]
level = "DEBUG"    # was "INFO"
```

### Option C — per-module at runtime

```python
import logging
logging.getLogger("compliance_bot.nl2sql_agent.sql_writer_agent").setLevel(logging.DEBUG)
```

---

## Logger Naming Convention

All loggers follow the dot-separated hierarchy:

```
compliance_bot                            ← root for all app loggers
compliance_bot.application                main.py
compliance_bot.core.model                 model.py
compliance_bot.core.processor             processor.py
compliance_bot.nl2sql_agent.graph         graph.py
compliance_bot.nl2sql_agent.intent_checker
compliance_bot.nl2sql_agent.schema_checker
compliance_bot.nl2sql_agent.sql_writer_agent
compliance_bot.nl2sql_agent.sql_reviewer
compliance_bot.nl2sql_agent.sql_validator
compliance_bot.nl2sql_agent.executor_agent
compliance_bot.utils.registry
```

Because `compliance_bot` is configured in `logging.toml` with `propagate = false`, all of these are captured by the `rotating_file` and `console` handlers automatically — you never need to configure child loggers individually.

---

## Reading Log Lines

```
2026-08-07 22:43:24 | INFO     | compliance_bot.nl2sql_agent.graph | graph:166 | NL2SQL LangGraph compiled successfully.
│                      │           │                                  │            │
│                      │           logger name                        file:line   message
│                      level
timestamp
```

### Stage trail in logs

Each agent node appends a string to the `stages` list. After a request completes you'll see something like:

```
pipeline complete — stages=['intent_checker:ok',
                             'schema_checker:db=COMP_REPORTS.db',
                             'sql_writer_agent:llm_generated retry=0',
                             'sql_reviewer:passed',
                             'sql_validator:passed dialect=sqlite',
                             'executor_agent:ok rows=12']
```

This is logged at `INFO` level by `ComplianceProcessor._map_state_to_response()`.

---

## Adding a Logger to a New Module

```python
from utils.logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.my_new_module")

# Use standard % formatting — never f-strings in log calls
LOGGER.info("Processing request: question=%r", question)
LOGGER.warning("Retrying — attempt %d/%d", attempt, max_retries)
LOGGER.exception("Unexpected error in %s", context)   # includes traceback
```

> **Do not use f-strings in log calls.** Use `%s` / `%r` / `%d` so the message is only formatted when the log level is actually enabled.



####################################################################################################
FILE: docs\running.md
####################################################################################################

# Running the Application

> **Golden rule:** Always run commands from the **project root** (`COMPLIANCE-CHATBOT-DLH-V2\`), never from inside `application\`.

---

## Prerequisites

```powershell
# Install all dependencies (once)
pip install -r requirements.txt

# Seed the database (once — creates data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db)
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --count 2000
```

---

## Interface 1 — Backend only (`main.py`)

The FastAPI backend with the full LangGraph pipeline.

```powershell
# Default port from .env (8080)
python application/main.py

# Override port (useful if 8080 is taken)
python application/main.py --port 8668

# Override host and port
python application/main.py --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8668
```

**What starts:**
- FastAPI server on `http://0.0.0.0:<PORT>`
- Swagger UI at `http://127.0.0.1:<PORT>/docs`
- LangGraph pipeline compiled in memory

**Stopping:** `Ctrl+C`

---

## Interface 2 — UI + Backend (`run_ui.py`)

The built-in HTTP UI (chat + LLM config page).

```powershell
# Terminal 1 — start the backend
python application/main.py

# Terminal 2 — start the UI (defaults to port 5001)
python application/run_ui.py
```

Then open: `http://127.0.0.1:5001`

**Environment variables for `run_ui.py`:**

```powershell
# Tell the UI where the backend is (default: http://127.0.0.1:8000)
$env:CB_BACKEND_URL = "http://127.0.0.1:8668"
python application/run_ui.py

# Change the UI port (default: 5001)
$env:CB_UI_PORT = "8100"
python application/run_ui.py
```

---

## Interface 3 — Launcher (both at once)

`Launcher.py` starts the FastAPI backend **and** the UI server together.

```powershell
# From project root
python application/Launcher.py
```

Or from inside `application\`:
```powershell
cd application
python Launcher.py
```

Launcher auto-detects the project root from its own file path — it works from either location.

**Default ports used by Launcher:**

| Service | Port |
|---------|------|
| FastAPI backend | 8001 |
| UI server | `$env:CDSW_APP_PORT` or 8100 |

**Stopping:** `Ctrl+C` — Launcher catches the interrupt and terminates both sub-processes cleanly.

---

## Interface 4 — uvicorn directly

Always run from the **project root**:

```powershell
# From COMPLIANCE-CHATBOT-DLH-V2\
uvicorn application.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8668

# With auto-reload for development
uvicorn application.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8668 --reload
```

**Why it fails when run from `application\`:**

```powershell
# ❌ WRONG — ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'application'
cd application
uvicorn application.main:app --port 8668
```

When you're inside `application\`, Python's module resolution looks for a package called `application` inside the current directory — but `application` IS the current directory, not a subdirectory. Running from the project root fixes this because `application/` is then a proper subdirectory on the path.

---

## Port Conflict — `[Errno 10048]`

If you see:
```
ERROR: [Errno 10048] error while attempting to bind on address ('0.0.0.0', 8080)
```

**Find and kill the process holding the port:**

```powershell
# Find which PID is using port 8080
netstat -ano | findstr :8080

# Kill it (replace 1234 with the actual PID)
taskkill /F /PID 1234
```

**Or just use a different port:**

```powershell
python application/main.py --port 8668
```

---

## CLI Entry-point (no FastAPI)

For debugging the pipeline in isolation without the HTTP server:

```powershell
python -m genai.nl2sql_agent.main "How many senior citizen accounts have KYC flag N?"
```

Outputs the full pipeline result as pretty-printed JSON to stdout.

---

## Jupyter Notebook

```powershell
jupyter notebook application/notebook.ipynb
```

The notebook contains a walkthrough of the pipeline with step-by-step cells for each agent node.

---

## Quick Reference

| Goal | Command (from project root) |
|------|-----------------------------|
| Start backend only | `python application/main.py` |
| Start backend on custom port | `python application/main.py --port 8668` |
| Start UI only | `python application/run_ui.py` |
| Start both together | `python application/Launcher.py` |
| Start via uvicorn | `uvicorn application.main:app --port 8668` |
| Run pipeline CLI | `python -m genai.nl2sql_agent.main "question"` |
| Seed database | `python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --count 2000` |
| Reset and re-seed | `python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 2000` |
| Run tests | `python -m pytest tests/ -v` |
| Lint | `python -m ruff check .` |

---

## Environment Variables

| Variable | Default | Used by |
|----------|---------|---------|
| `CB_BACKEND_URL` | `http://127.0.0.1:8000` | `run_ui.py` — where to proxy API requests |
| `CB_UI_PORT` | `5001` | `run_ui.py` — port for the UI server |
| `CDSW_APP_PORT` | `8100` | `Launcher.py` — CML app port |
| `PORT` | from `.env` | `AppSettings` — FastAPI bind port |
| `HOST` | `0.0.0.0` | `AppSettings` — FastAPI bind host |

# Testing

The test suite uses **pytest** and covers all modules that don't require a live LLM or network connection.

---

## Running Tests

```powershell
# From project root

# Run the full suite
python -m pytest tests/ -v

# Run quietly (just pass/fail counts)
python -m pytest tests/ -q

# Run a single file
python -m pytest tests/test_intent_checker.py -v

# Run a specific test
python -m pytest tests/test_intent_checker.py::TestTooShort::test_single_word -v

# Stop on first failure
python -m pytest tests/ -x

# Show local variables on failure
python -m pytest tests/ --tb=long
```

---

## Test Files

| File | Module tested | Tests |
|------|--------------|-------|
| `test_utils_helpers.py` | `utils/helpers.py` | JSON helpers, file I/O, truncate, flatten |
| `test_utils_config.py` | `utils/config.py` | AppSettings, ModelSettings, DatabaseSettings |
| `test_utils_logger.py` | `utils/logger.py` | `get_logger`, `_LOGGING_CONFIGURED` flag |
| `test_utils_registry.py` | `utils/registry.py` | `load_prompt`, `get_prompt`, cache hit |
| `test_exceptions.py` | `genai/core/exceptions.py` | Exception hierarchy, message storage |
| `test_schemas.py` | `genai/core/schemas.py` | Pydantic models, factory helpers |
| `test_intent_checker.py` | `genai/nl2sql_agent/intent_checker.py` | All 4 intent rules |
| `test_sql_validator.py` | `genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_validator.py` | sqlglot AST checks |
| `test_sql_reviewer.py` | `genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_reviewer.py` | Regex + schema safety checks |
| `test_executor_agent.py` | `genai/nl2sql_agent/executor_agent.py` | SQLite execution vs `tmp_db` |
| `test_executor_helpers.py` | `executor_agent` helpers | `format_summary`, `to_markdown_table`, `to_csv` |
| `test_schema_checker.py` | `genai/nl2sql_agent/schema_checker.py` | Table introspection vs `tmp_db` |

---

## Shared Fixtures (`tests/conftest.py`)

### `tmp_db`

A temporary SQLite database pre-seeded with 5 `COMP_TBL` rows.  
Scope: `session` (created once, shared across all tests).

```python
@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def tmp_db(tmp_path_factory) -> Path:
    """Returns Path to a temp SQLite file with 5 seeded rows."""
```

Use it in any test:

```python
def test_row_count(tmp_db):
    from utils.registry import row_count
    assert row_count(tmp_db, "COMP_TBL") == 5
```

### `tmp_prompt_dir`

A temporary directory with minimal YAML prompt files for `intent_checker`, `schema_checker`, and `sql_review`.  
Used to test the registry without depending on the real `genai/prompt/` files.

```python
@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def tmp_prompt_dir(tmp_path_factory) -> Path:
    """Returns Path to a temp dir containing minimal YAML prompt files."""
```

Use it with `monkeypatch` to redirect the registry:

```python
def test_custom_prompt(tmp_prompt_dir, monkeypatch):
    import utils.registry as reg
    monkeypatch.setattr(reg, "_PROMPTS_DIR", tmp_prompt_dir)
    monkeypatch.setattr(reg, "_prompt_cache", {})
    val = reg.get_prompt("intent_checker.yaml", "messages", "empty_question")
    assert val == "No question provided."
```

---

## Adding Tests for a New Module

1. Create `tests/test_<module_name>.py`.
2. Import the module under test using the full package path:

```python
from genai.nl2sql_agent.my_node import MyNode
from genai.nl2sql_agent.state import AgentState
```

3. Use `tmp_db` or `tmp_prompt_dir` fixtures as needed.
4. Follow the existing class-per-scenario pattern:

```python
class TestMyNodeHappyPath:
    def test_basic_input(self):
        node = MyNode()
        state = AgentState(question="How many accounts?")
        result = node.run(state)
        assert result["my_output_key"] == expected_value

class TestMyNodeEdgeCases:
    def test_empty_input(self):
        node = MyNode()
        result = node.run(AgentState(question=""))
        assert result.get("error") is not None
```

---

## pytest Configuration (`pyproject.toml`)

```toml
[tool.pytest.ini_options]
testpaths         = ["tests"]
addopts           = "--strict-markers -v --tb=short -p no:spark --import-mode=importlib"
asyncio_mode      = "auto"
pythonpath        = ["."]
```

Key settings:

| Setting | Reason |
|---------|--------|
| `--import-mode=importlib` | Prevents pytest from trying to import the root-level files as a package |
| `pythonpath = ["."]` | Adds project root to `sys.path` so `from utils.xxx import ...` works |
| `asyncio_mode = "auto"` | All `async def test_*` functions run automatically without `@pytest.mark.asyncio` |

---

## Linting (ruff)

```powershell
# Check all files
python -m ruff check .

# Auto-fix fixable issues
python -m ruff check . --fix
```

All 198 tests pass and ruff returns `All checks passed!` on the main branch.

---

## What Is NOT Tested (and Why)

| Module | Reason not unit-tested |
|--------|----------------------|
| `genai/core/model.py` — `ModelLoaderService.generate()` | Requires a live DLH inference server |
| `genai/nl2sql_agent/sql_writer_agent.py` — `SQLWriterAgent.run()` | Requires a live LLM |
| `genai/nl2sql_agent/graph.py` — end-to-end `graph.invoke()` | Requires a live LLM |
| `application/main.py` — FastAPI endpoints | Would require an ASGI test client + mock LLM |

To add integration tests for the FastAPI layer, use `httpx.AsyncClient` with the FastAPI test app:

```python
import pytest
from httpx import AsyncClient, ASGITransport
from application.main import app

@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_health():
    async with AsyncClient(transport=ASGITransport(app=app), base_url="http://test") as client:
        r = await client.get("/health")
    assert r.status_code == 200
    assert r.json()["status"] == "ok"
```

####################################################################################################
FILE: genai\core\exceptions.py
####################################################################################################



"""
genai/core/exceptions.py — domain exception hierarchy for the Compliance Chatbot.

All application-specific exceptions inherit from ComplianceBotError so
callers can catch them at a single abstraction level.  Internal stack traces
are never leaked to API clients.
"""


class ComplianceBotError(Exception):
    """Base exception for all Compliance Chatbot domain errors."""

    def __init__(self, message: str, detail: str | None = None) -> None:
        super().__init__(message)
        self.message: str = message
        self.detail: str | None = detail

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        return self.message



class ModelLoadError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when an LLM or tokenizer fails to load."""


class ModelInferenceError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when the model produces an unexpected output during inference."""


class LLMProviderError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when an LLM provider call fails (auth, HTTP error, bad response …)."""


class LLMTimeoutError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when an LLM provider call exceeds the configured timeout."""


class UnknownProviderError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when an unrecognised LLM provider name is requested."""



class SqlGenerationError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when the NL-to-SQL pipeline cannot produce a valid SQL statement."""


class SqlExecutionError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when a generated SQL query fails to execute against the database."""



class DatabaseConnectionError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when the application cannot connect to the database."""


class RecordNotFoundError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when an expected database record is absent."""



class DataIngestionError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when raw data cannot be ingested or structured correctly."""


class SchemaValidationError(ComplianceBotError):
    """Raised when an incoming payload fails schema validation."""



####################################################################################################
FILE: genai\core\model.py
####################################################################################################



"""
genai/core/model.py — LLM provider lifecycle, inference, and management.


All LLM-related code lives here; nothing else in genai/core/ touches the
OpenAI client directly.
"""

import re
import time

import httpx
from openai import APIError, APITimeoutError, OpenAI

from genai.core.exceptions import (
    ModelInferenceError,
    ModelLoadError,
    UnknownProviderError,
)
from utils.config import (
    DEFAULT_MAX_NEW_TOKENS,
    DEFAULT_TEMPERATURE,
    SQL_BEGIN_TAG,
    SQL_END_TAG,
    ModelSettings,
    get_settings,
)
from utils.logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.core.model")


class ModelLoaderService:
    """Manages the lifecycle of the configured remote LLM provider."""

    def __init__(self, settings: ModelSettings) -> None:
        self.settings: ModelSettings = settings
        self._client: OpenAI | None = None
        self._send_sampling_params = True

    def load(self) -> None:
        """Load the configured provider resources."""
        self._load_dlh_client()

    def _load_dlh_client(self) -> None:
        """Initialise the DLH OpenAI-compatible client."""
        http_client = None
        if not self.settings.dlh_verify_ssl:
            http_client = httpx.Client(verify=False)  # noqa: S501
        try:
            self._client = OpenAI(
                api_key=self.settings.dlh_api_key,
                base_url=self.settings.dlh_base_url,
                timeout=30.0,
                **({"http_client": http_client} if http_client is not None else {}),
            )
            LOGGER.info("DLH client initialised successfully.")
        except Exception as exc:  # pragma: no cover
            raise ModelLoadError(f"Failed to initialise DLH client: {exc}", detail=str(exc)) from exc

    def is_loaded(self) -> bool:
        """Return True when the configured provider is ready for inference."""
        return self._client is not None

    def test_connection(self) -> None:
        """Verify the configured remote provider connection with a minimal request."""
        if self._client is None:
            raise ModelLoadError("Remote client is not initialised.")

        request_kwargs = {
            "model": self.settings.model_name,
            "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "Reply with OK."}],
            "temperature": 0.1,
            "max_tokens":  8,
        }

        try:
            self._client.chat.completions.create(**request_kwargs)
        except APITimeoutError as exc:
            raise ModelInferenceError(f"Connection test timed out: {exc}", detail=str(exc)) from exc
        except APIError as exc:
            raise ModelInferenceError(f"Connection test failed: {exc}", detail=str(exc)) from exc

    def generate(
        self,
        prompt: str,
        max_new_tokens: int = DEFAULT_MAX_NEW_TOKENS,
        temperature: float = DEFAULT_TEMPERATURE,
    ) -> str:
        """Run inference on the configured provider."""
        if not self.is_loaded():
            raise ModelInferenceError(
                "Model is not loaded. Call ModelLoaderService.load() before generating."
            )

        request_kwargs = {
            "model": self.settings.model_name,
            "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": prompt}],
            "temperature": temperature,
            "max_tokens":  max_new_tokens,
        }

        try:
            response = self._client.chat.completions.create(**request_kwargs)
            return response.choices[0].message.content or ""
        except APITimeoutError as exc:
            raise ModelInferenceError(f"Provider request timed out: {exc}", detail=str(exc)) from exc
        except APIError as exc:
            raise ModelInferenceError(f"Provider API error: {exc}", detail=str(exc)) from exc

    def extract_sql(self, model_output: str) -> str | None:
        """Extract the SQL statement from delimited model output."""
        pattern = re.compile(
            rf"{re.escape(SQL_BEGIN_TAG)}\s*(.*?)\s*{re.escape(SQL_END_TAG)}",
            re.DOTALL | re.IGNORECASE,
        )
        match = pattern.search(model_output)
        if match:
            sql = match.group(1).strip()
            LOGGER.debug("Extracted SQL: %s", sql)
            return sql
        LOGGER.warning("SQL delimiter tags not found in model output. Raw: %s", model_output[:300])
        return None

    def unload(self) -> None:
        """Release provider resources from memory."""
        LOGGER.info("Unloading model resources …")
        self._client = None
        LOGGER.info("Model unloaded.")



def create_model_service(settings: "ModelSettings | None" = None) -> ModelLoaderService:
    """
    Build and load a ModelLoaderService, then return it ready for inference.

    Pass a ModelSettings object to override the defaults, or leave as None
    to use whatever is configured in .env / AppSettings.
    """
    resolved = settings or get_settings().model
    LOGGER.info(
        "create_model_service — provider=%s model=%s",
        resolved.provider, resolved.model_name,
    )
    service = ModelLoaderService(settings=resolved)
    service.load()
    return service



def generate_with_retry(
    model_service: ModelLoaderService,
    prompt: str,
    max_new_tokens: int = DEFAULT_MAX_NEW_TOKENS,
    temperature: float = DEFAULT_TEMPERATURE,
    retries: int = 2,
    retry_delay: float = 1.0,
) -> str:
    """
    Call model_service.generate() and retry on failure.

    retries — how many additional attempts after the first (total = retries + 1).
    Raises ModelInferenceError if every attempt fails.
    """
    last_exc: ModelInferenceError | None = None
    for attempt in range(retries + 1):
        try:
            return model_service.generate(
                prompt=prompt,
                max_new_tokens=max_new_tokens,
                temperature=temperature,
            )
        except ModelInferenceError as exc:
            last_exc = exc
            if attempt < retries:
                LOGGER.warning(
                    "generate_with_retry — attempt %d/%d failed: %s. Retrying in %.1fs…",
                    attempt + 1, retries + 1, exc, retry_delay,
                )
                time.sleep(retry_delay)

    raise last_exc  # type: ignore[misc]


def extract_text_between(text: str, start_marker: str, end_marker: str) -> str | None:
    """
    Pull the content between start_marker and end_marker out of text.

    Case-insensitive.  Returns None if either marker is missing.
    """
    lower = text.lower()
    start_idx = lower.find(start_marker.lower())
    if start_idx == -1:
        return None
    content_start = start_idx + len(start_marker)
    end_idx = lower.find(end_marker.lower(), content_start)
    if end_idx == -1:
        return None
    return text[content_start:end_idx].strip()


def truncate_prompt(prompt: str, max_chars: int = 8000) -> str:
    """
    Keep the prompt under max_chars by removing characters from the middle.

    Preserving the header (instructions) and the tail (question) gives the
    LLM the most important context even when the schema block is very long.
    """
    if len(prompt) <= max_chars:
        return prompt
    half = max_chars // 2
    LOGGER.warning("truncate_prompt — length %d > %d; cutting the middle.", len(prompt), max_chars)
    return prompt[:half] + "\n...[truncated]...\n" + prompt[-half:]



class ProviderManager:
    """
    Registry and lifecycle manager for multiple LLM provider services.

    Register providers first, then call activate() to make one of them live.
    The active provider is the one all inference calls go through.
    """

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self._registry: dict[str, ModelSettings] = {}
        self._services: dict[str, ModelLoaderService] = {}
        self._active_name: str | None = None

    def register(self, name: str, settings: ModelSettings) -> None:
        """Store a provider configuration under name (not loaded until activate())."""
        self._registry[name] = settings
        LOGGER.info("ProviderManager — registered provider: %s", name)

    def activate(self, name: str) -> ModelLoaderService:
        """Make name the active provider, loading it if needed."""
        if name not in self._registry:
            raise UnknownProviderError(
                f"Provider '{name}' is not registered. Available: {list(self._registry)}"
            )
        if name not in self._services:
            service = ModelLoaderService(settings=self._registry[name])
            service.load()
            self._services[name] = service
            LOGGER.info("ProviderManager — loaded provider: %s", name)
        self._active_name = name
        LOGGER.info("ProviderManager — active provider: %s", name)
        return self._services[name]

    @property
    def active(self) -> ModelLoaderService:
        """Return the currently active service. Raises RuntimeError if none is set."""
        if self._active_name is None or self._active_name not in self._services:
            raise RuntimeError("No active provider. Call ProviderManager.activate(name) first.")
        return self._services[self._active_name]

    @property
    def active_name(self) -> str | None:
        """Name of the currently active provider, or None."""
        return self._active_name

    def registered_names(self) -> list[str]:
        """Return a sorted list of registered provider names."""
        return sorted(self._registry)

    def unload(self, name: str) -> None:
        """Unload a provider service and free its memory."""
        service = self._services.pop(name, None)
        if service is not None:
            service.unload()
            LOGGER.info("ProviderManager — unloaded provider: %s", name)
        if self._active_name == name:
            self._active_name = None

    def unload_all(self) -> None:
        """Unload every registered provider service."""
        for name in list(self._services):
            self.unload(name)



####################################################################################################
FILE: genai\core\processor.py
####################################################################################################



"""
genai/core/processor.py — compliance orchestration and legacy schema reference.

 — integration point between FastAPI and LangGraph
 — legacy Python schema dict (kept as reference)

ComplianceProcessor is the single entry-point for all compliance queries:
it accepts a ComplianceChatRequest, invokes the compiled LangGraph graph,
and maps the resulting AgentState onto the Pydantic response models.
"""

import uuid
from typing import Any

from genai.core.exceptions import ComplianceBotError
from genai.core.schemas import (
    ComplianceChatRequest,
    ComplianceChatResponse,
    ComplianceOrchestrationResponse,
)
from genai.nl2sql_agent.state import AgentState
from utils.logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.core.processor")



class ComplianceProcessor:
    """
    Orchestrates the NL2SQL compliance query pipeline via LangGraph.

    Intentionally thin — all multi-agent logic lives inside the agent nodes
    and graph wiring.  This class maps between API models and AgentState.
    """

    def __init__(self, compiled_graph: Any) -> None:
        self._graph = compiled_graph

    def process_query(self, request: ComplianceChatRequest) -> ComplianceChatResponse:
        """Run the agentic pipeline and return the simplified chat response."""
        orchestration = self.process_query_with_orchestration(request)
        return ComplianceChatResponse(
            question_id=orchestration.question_id,
            sql_query=orchestration.sql_query,
            df_output=orchestration.df_output,
            result_columns=orchestration.result_columns,
            result_summary=orchestration.result_summary,
            error=orchestration.error or orchestration.clarification_question,
        )

    def process_query_with_orchestration(
        self,
        request: ComplianceChatRequest,
    ) -> ComplianceOrchestrationResponse:
        """Run the full LangGraph NL2SQL pipeline and return the detailed response."""
        question_id = request.question_id or str(uuid.uuid4())
        question = (request.question or "").strip()

        if not question:
            LOGGER.warning("[%s] Empty question received.", question_id)
            return ComplianceOrchestrationResponse(
                question_id=question_id,
                error="No question was provided in the request.",
                stages=["validation:empty_question"],
            )

        LOGGER.info("[%s] Invoking NL2SQL LangGraph for: %r", question_id, question)

        initial_state: AgentState = AgentState(  # type: ignore[misc]
            question=question,
            question_id=question_id,
            stages=[],
        )

        try:
            final_state: AgentState = self._graph.invoke(initial_state)
        except ComplianceBotError:
            raise
        except Exception:  # pylint: disable=broad-exception-caught
            LOGGER.exception("[%s] Unexpected graph error", question_id)
            return ComplianceOrchestrationResponse(
                question_id=question_id,
                error="Unexpected pipeline error — see server logs for details.",
                stages=["graph:unexpected_error"],
            )

        return self._map_state_to_response(question_id, final_state)

    @staticmethod
    def _map_state_to_response(
        question_id: str,
        state: AgentState,
    ) -> ComplianceOrchestrationResponse:
        """Convert the final AgentState into a ComplianceOrchestrationResponse."""
        clarification = state.get("clarification_needed")
        sql_query      = state.get("sql_query") or None
        result_rows    = state.get("result_rows") or None
        result_columns = list(state.get("result_columns") or [])
        result_summary = state.get("result_summary") or None
        error          = state.get("error") or None
        stages         = list(state.get("stages", []))
        interpreted    = state.get("interpreted_question") or state.get("question", "")

        LOGGER.info(
            "[%s] Pipeline complete — stages=%s rows=%s error=%s",
            question_id, stages, len(result_rows) if result_rows else 0, error,
        )

        return ComplianceOrchestrationResponse(
            question_id=question_id,
            interpreted_question=interpreted,
            needs_clarification=bool(clarification),
            clarification_question=clarification,
            sql_query=sql_query,
            df_output=result_rows,
            result_columns=result_columns,
            result_summary=result_summary,
            stages=stages,
            error=error,
        )

####################################################################################################
FILE: genai\core\schemas.py
####################################################################################################



"""
genai/core/schemas.py — Pydantic API models and response factory functions.

— Pydantic request / response models (API contract)
— factory helpers that construct those models

These are the single source of truth for the API contract.  Keeping the
factories here means callers never repeat the same keyword arguments.
"""

from typing import Any

from pydantic import BaseModel, Field, field_validator


class ComplianceChatRequest(BaseModel):
    """Payload accepted by the chat and orchestrate endpoints."""

    question_id: str | None = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Optional caller-supplied idempotency key.",
        examples=["req-001"],
    )
    question: str | None = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Natural-language compliance question.",
        examples=["Show customers whose age is more than 60 and KYC flag is Y."],
    )

    @field_validator("question")
    @classmethod
    def question_must_not_be_blank(cls, value: str | None) -> str | None:
        """Reject blank strings; strip valid non-null values."""
        if value is not None and not value.strip():
            raise ValueError("'question' must not be a blank string.")
        return value.strip() if value else value


class ComplianceChatResponse(BaseModel):
    """Simplified response returned by the /chat endpoint."""

    question_id: str | None = Field(default=None)
    sql_query: str | None = Field(default=None)
    df_output: list[dict[str, object]] | None = Field(default=None)
    result_columns: list[str] = Field(default_factory=list)
    result_summary: str | None = Field(default=None)
    error: str | None = Field(default=None)


class LLMConnectionRequest(BaseModel):
    """Payload for configuring and testing a DLH LLM connection."""

    provider: str = Field(default="dlh", description="LLM provider (dlh).")
    model_name: str | None = Field(default=None)
    dlh_base_url: str | None = Field(default=None)
    dlh_api_key: str | None = Field(default=None)
    dlh_verify_ssl: bool = Field(default=False)


class LLMConnectionResponse(BaseModel):
    """Response for the LLM configuration and connection-test endpoint."""

    success: bool = Field(description="Whether the connection test succeeded.")
    provider: str = Field(description="Configured LLM provider name.")
    message: str  = Field(description="Human-readable connection status message.")


class ComplianceOrchestrationResponse(BaseModel):
    """
    Extended response returned by the /orchestrate endpoint.

    Includes the full agent stage audit trail, interpreted question, SQL,
    result rows with column metadata, and any clarification or error detail.
    """

    question_id: str | None = Field(default=None)
    interpreted_question: str | None = Field(default=None)
    needs_clarification: bool = Field(default=False)
    clarification_question: str | None = Field(default=None)
    sql_query: str | None = Field(default=None)
    df_output: list[dict[str, object]] | None = Field(default=None)
    result_columns: list[str] = Field(default_factory=list)
    result_summary: str | None = Field(default=None)
    stages: list[str] = Field(default_factory=list)
    error: str | None = Field(default=None)


class HealthResponse(BaseModel):
    """Response payload for the /health liveness probe."""

    status: str  = Field(default="ok")
    version: str = Field(default="2.0.0")



def build_chat_response(
    question_id: str | None,
    sql_query: str | None,
    df_output: list[dict[str, Any]] | None,
    result_columns: list[str] | None,
    result_summary: str | None,
    error: str | None,
) -> ComplianceChatResponse:
    """Build the simplified chat response from individual pipeline outputs."""
    return ComplianceChatResponse(
        question_id=question_id,
        sql_query=sql_query,
        df_output=df_output,
        result_columns=result_columns or [],
        result_summary=result_summary,
        error=error,
    )


def build_orchestration_response(
    question_id: str | None,
    interpreted_question: str | None = None,
    needs_clarification: bool = False,
    clarification_question: str | None = None,
    sql_query: str | None = None,
    df_output: list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
    result_columns: list[str] | None = None,
    result_summary: str | None = None,
    stages: list[str] | None = None,
    error: str | None = None,
) -> ComplianceOrchestrationResponse:
    """Build the full orchestration response from individual pipeline outputs."""
    return ComplianceOrchestrationResponse(
        question_id=question_id,
        interpreted_question=interpreted_question,
        needs_clarification=needs_clarification,
        clarification_question=clarification_question,
        sql_query=sql_query,
        df_output=df_output,
        result_columns=result_columns or [],
        result_summary=result_summary,
        stages=stages or [],
        error=error,
    )


def build_error_response(
    question_id: str | None,
    error_message: str,
    stage_tag: str = "pipeline:error",
) -> ComplianceOrchestrationResponse:
    """Build a minimal error response with only the error field and stage tag set."""
    return ComplianceOrchestrationResponse(
        question_id=question_id,
        error=error_message,
        stages=[stage_tag],
    )


def build_health_response(version: str = "2.0.0") -> HealthResponse:
    """Return a healthy HealthResponse with status='ok'."""
    return HealthResponse(status="ok", version=version)


def build_llm_connection_response(
    success: bool,
    provider: str,
    message: str,
) -> LLMConnectionResponse:
    """Build a connection-test response indicating success or failure."""
    return LLMConnectionResponse(success=success, provider=provider, message=message)
